Um... well, I really try not to let myself think that way, because I know it doesn't help anything. I try to stay positive and focus on how to fix things, you know?

Um... well, as a choreographer, I always try to stay really active and keep the energy up in the room. I don't want to bring anyone down or seem like I'm not giving it my all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really try to keep a good schedule, you know? Sleep is so important for recovery, especially for dancers, and I always tell my team to make sure they're resting. I don't want to seem like I'm not practicing what I preach. 

But... I guess my sleep has been a little all over the place lately? I feel bad admitting it, because I know I should be managing it better. Like, before the performances were cancelled, I was just so inspired that I'd stay up sketching formations until dawn. I felt fine then, I promise, I was just really dedicated to the work. 

But lately... I don't know, I fall asleep completely exhausted, but then I just pop awake at four or five in the morning, and my stomach is just in knots. It's so tight. I really try to go back to sleep, I close my eyes and do deep breathing and everything, but I just can't. I hope you don't think I'm just having bad sleep hygiene or staying up looking at my phone or something. Is it okay that my sleep is a little messed up right now, with everything going on?

Do you think it's okay that I'm feeling a little guilty right now, just while we're trying to figure everything out?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or that you can’t feel any pleasure from them anymore, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep doing the things I love, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not completely. I really want to be clear about that, because I don't want you to think I'm just totally shut down or not trying to find the good in my days. I know how important it is to keep a positive outlook.

I mean, I always try to find at least one little thing to appreciate every day, even if it's just a nice cup of tea or seeing the sun come through the window. I promise I haven't had days where there's just... absolutely nothing. It's more like I was saying before, where the joy is just a little dimmer right now. But I'm still feeling *something*, I really am. I wouldn't let myself just completely give up on finding pleasure in life, you know? That wouldn't be healthy at all. 

So, no, I wouldn't say there have been days with zero joy. I'm doing my best to keep my spirits up. Does that make sense? I hope that's a good sign that I'm still trying my best to stay engaged with everything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh, no. Never meaningless. I mean... my friends are my rock, and dance is my whole life. I would never want you to think I don't value them anymore, or that I'm just totally checked out. 

Even if I'm having a slower week where things feel a bit muted, like we talked about, I still know exactly how much they matter to me. It's never felt empty. I really make sure to remind myself how lucky I am to have my friends and my career, even on the days when I have to push myself a little harder to get out the door. I would feel just awful if I ever let myself think of them as meaningless. 

I hope that shows I'm keeping a good perspective on things? I really don't want to come across as ungrateful or, you know, totally detached from my life, because I'm definitely not. I'm trying really hard to stay grounded in what's important. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it?

Um... well, I don't know if I would use the word depressed, just because that sounds so severe, and I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just giving up. I promise I'm trying my hardest to keep a good attitude.

I'm still trying my absolute best to stay engaged and enjoy it, even if I feel a little heavy inside. Is it okay if I haven't noticed a change with that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, and I definitely haven't stopped doing the things I love.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you're lacking energy more often than usual, even if you're still doing your work and trying to stay engaged?\n\nPatient: Um, well, maybe a little bit? I guess I have noticed that I feel pretty wiped out after doing just really small things lately. I just feel awful complaining about being tired, and I don't want you to think I'm not trying. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I know we touched on this a little bit a minute ago, but no, I really haven't lost the pleasure in them. I wouldn't say that at all. 

I mean, like I mentioned, with that heavy feeling lately, sometimes it takes a little more energy to actually get myself to the studio or to get ready to meet up with people. But once I'm actually there? I still love it. I really do. Choreographing still gives me that same spark, and seeing my friends still makes me smile. 

I always try to focus on the good parts of my day, and I make sure to really appreciate those moments when I'm doing what I love. I haven't lost that joy at all. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just brushing off your questions, I just... I want to be completely honest that I still really enjoy the things I care about. That's a good sign, isn't it? That I'm still finding the joy in things?

I would feel just awful if I ever let myself think of them as meaningless. \n\nI hope that shows I'm keeping a good perspective on things? I really don't want to come across as ungrateful or, you know, totally detached from my life, because I'm definitely not. I'm trying really hard to stay grounded in what's important. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I don't know if I would use the word depressed, just because that sounds so severe, and I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just giving up. Is it okay that my appetite is just a little off right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual or, on the contrary, that you feel restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Um... well, as a choreographer, I always try to stay really active and keep the energy up in the room. I don't want to bring anyone down or seem like I'm not giving it my all. \n\nBut... I guess I have noticed I'm not quite as quick on my feet as I usually am? I feel a little embarrassed even admitting it, because I really don't want to seem lazy. But recently, even just doing something simple, like... crossing the studio to pick up my notebook, it just feels so slowed down. It's almost like my body is moving through wet sand instead of music, if that makes sense? \n\nI'm really trying to push through it, though, I promise. I keep trying to stretch and warm up like normal. I hope you don't think I'm just slacking off or not trying hard enough at work. Is it normal to feel that heavy and sluggish when you're going through a rough patch?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up too early, or sleeping much more than usual even if you don’t feel like you need to?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep a good schedule, you know? Sleep is so important for recovery, especially for dancers, and I always tell my team to make sure they're resting. I don't want to seem like I'm not practicing what I preach. \n\nBut... I guess my sleep has been a little all over the place lately? I feel bad admitting it, because I know I should be managing it better. Like, before the performances were cancelled, I was just so inspired that I'd stay up sketching formations until dawn. I felt fine then, I promise, I was just really dedicated to the work. \n\nBut lately... I don't know, I fall asleep completely exhausted, but then I just pop awake at four or five in the morning, and my stomach is just in knots. It's so tight. I really try to go back to sleep, I close my eyes and do deep breathing and everything, but I just can't. I hope you don't think I'm just having bad sleep hygiene or staying up looking at my phone or something. Is it okay that my sleep is a little messed up right now, with everything going on?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling worse about who you are or feeling like you don’t measure up to the person you want to be lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep a positive mindset. As a choreographer, I know I need to be a strong role model and project confidence for the company. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm just wallowing or feeling sorry for myself, you know? \n\nBut... I guess I have been struggling a little bit with feeling like I'm not... doing a good enough job? I feel awful even admitting it out loud, because everyone has been so incredibly supportive. It's just... I keep replaying the cancelled performances over and over in my head. I really try to just let it go and look forward, I promise, but I keep telling myself that I completely let everyone down. The dancers who worked so hard, the funders, the whole community... \n\nAnd honestly, people have been so much kinder about it than I actually deserve. They've been so sweet, which almost makes me feel worse, if that makes sense? I'm really trying hard to practice self-compassion and remind myself that some things are just out of my control. I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or not trying to move on. Do you think it's okay that I'm feeling a little guilty right now, just while we're trying to figure everything out?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or that you can’t feel any pleasure from them anymore, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep doing the things I love, you know? I think it's so important to stay engaged and keep finding joy in the little things, especially when times are a bit tough. I don't want to be someone who just gives up on their passions.\n\nBut... honestly, I haven't completely lost interest in everything. I still try to listen to music, and I still love the *idea* of dance. I promise I'm still trying to do things that make me happy. It's just... maybe I don't get that same spark right now? Like, I'll put on a favorite track, and I know I *should* feel that rush to move, but it just feels a little flat at the moment. \n\nI'm still making the effort, though, I really am. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I know it's important to keep trying. Is it normal to just feel a little disconnected from your hobbies for a little while, as long as you're still trying?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days where nothing—not even things you usually love—has brought you any sense of pleasure or joy at all?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not completely. But I'm still feeling *something*, I really am. I wouldn't let myself just completely give up on finding pleasure in life, you know? That wouldn't be healthy at all. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say there have been days with zero joy. I'm doing my best to keep my spirits up. Does that make sense? I hope that's a good sign that I'm still trying my best to stay engaged with everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that even when you make yourself do things you usually enjoy, like choreographing or spending time with friends, you don’t feel any pleasure from them anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I mentioned, I really am trying my best to stay connected to the things I love. I don't want to sound like I'm just going through the motions and getting absolutely nothing out of it, because I know how important it is to keep trying. \n\nWhen I make myself work on some choreography or, you know, see friends, it's not like I feel completely numb or empty. I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when even the things you usually care about the most, like choreographing or seeing your closest friends, have felt completely empty or meaningless to you, even when you're trying your hardest to engage with them?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I think my concentration is pretty good. 

I guess sometimes, if I'm feeling really tired, my mind might wander just a tiny bit, but nothing major. I can always pull myself back to what I'm doing.
